Extra proofs (2 theorems from expanded bank)

Proof 1: Sard's theorem: critical values of smooth map have measure zero

Statement: Sard's theorem: critical values of smooth map have measure zero

Hypotheses: f: M -> N smooth, critical points where df is not surjective

Conclusion: f(critical points) has measure 0 in N

Definitions: Sard, critical value, measure zero

Proof:

Step 1: Induct on dimensions of domain and target

Step 2: Use Taylor expansion at critical points

Step 3: Measure of critical values in each coordinate chart vanishes

Step 4: Glue by countable additivity

Proof 2: Transversality theorem: generic perturbation is transverse

Statement: Transversality theorem: generic perturbation is transverse

Hypotheses: f: M -> N smooth, S submanifold of N

Conclusion: for generic perturbation g, g is transverse to S

Definitions: transversality, generic, perturbation

Proof:

Step 1: Use Thom transversality theorem

Step 2: Jet space construction

Step 3: Generic maps are dense in C-infinity

Step 4: Small perturbation achieves transversality

ID: defb9c2c04b6 | Status: UNFORMALIZED | Complexity: O(n^2)

Mutation test: Fundamental tool in differential topology

Provenance: Domain Differential, theorem #2

Extra proofs (+1 from domain theorem bank)

Proof 1: Differential forms: d^2=0 (exterior derivative squares to zero)

Statement: Differential forms: d^2=0 (exterior derivative squares to zero)

Hypotheses: smooth manifold

Conclusion: d(d omega) = 0 for any form omega

Definitions: exterior derivative, d^2=0, Poincare lemma

Proof:

Step 1: By definition: d is derivation of degree +1

Step 2: d(omega) = sum partial_i omega_j dx^i wedge dx_j

Step 3: Second derivative: mixed partials cancel

Step 4: d^2 omega = 0 identically
